--- Page 3 ---
<meta>This page serves as the official archival cover or title label for the manuscript, identifying it within the collection of the Shri Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute. ### No. 836 - Gh

# **<term>Muhūrtacintāmaṇi</term>** (<term>Astrology</term>)
<note>The title translates to "The Thought-Gem of Auspicious Moments." Composed by Rama Daivajña in the late 16th century, this is a foundational text in Hindu electional astrology (<term>Jyotiṣam</term>), used to calculate the most spiritually and cosmically favorable times for various life events and rituals.</note>

## 31 Folios (Complete)
<note>original: "Patrāṇi 31 (Sampūrṇam)." This archival note confirms that the manuscript consists of 31 individual leaves (folios) and that the text is entirely preserved from beginning to end.</note>

<summary>This archival label identifies manuscript number 836-Gh as a complete, 31-folio copy of the Muhūrtacintāmaṇi, a definitive Sanskrit treatise on electional astrology.</summary>
<keywords>Muhūrtacintāmaṇi, Jyotiṣam, Sanskrit manuscript, archival records, Vedic astrology, Rama Daivajña, Shri Raghunath Institute</keywords>

# **Page 531**

<note>The manuscript leaf shows significant aging, discoloration, and a large water stain on the left. Marginal notes in the upper-left corner are in a different hand and are very faded.</note>

->**The Thought-Gem of Auspicious Moments**<-

<note>The <term>Muhūrtacintāmaṇi</term> is one of the most significant works in <term>Jyotiṣa</term> (Vedic astrology). It provides a systematic guide for determining the "Muhūrta"—the most favorable astrological time—to perform activities such as weddings, house-warming ceremonies, or religious initiations. The author, Rama Daivajña, was a prominent scholar in the court of the Mughal Emperor Akbar.</note>

<summary>This page serves as the title leaf for a 1782 CE copy of the Muhūrtacintāmaṇi, featuring marginal notes that identify the author, the date of transcription, and the inclusion of a commentary.</summary>
<keywords>Muhūrtacintāmaṇi, Rama Daivajña, electional astrology, Vikrama Samvat 1839, Sanskrit manuscript, Jyotiṣa, astrological timing</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues from the title leaf (Folio 35). It marks the formal beginning of the manuscript, opening with a traditional invocation (mangalacharana) to the elephant-headed god Ganesha and the Sun god, followed by the first two verses of the Muhurtachintamani and the start of the technical rules regarding lunar days (tithis).</meta>

<note>This introductory section explains the mythological image of Ganesha playing with a leaf from his mother's ear, which serves as a poetic opening to the scholarly work. It then defines the purpose of the book: to guide the reader through the "play" of the world by identifying the correct times for rituals.</note>

By this [action], he does not swallow the <term>Ketaka</term> leaf <note>original: "ketaka-patra"; a fragrant screw-pine leaf</note> at the time of placing it in his mouth; just so, he is seen by the people. <gloss>The rituals beginning with the birth-ceremony, etc.</gloss> Their collection or group. <gloss>Knowledge of the time</gloss> which takes the form of "this should be done on this auspicious day, and this should not be done on that day," and the cause or reason for that [knowledge]. <gloss>This world is filled with the essence of the play of the soul's purpose.</gloss> The manifestation of that action is its "play." Its essence is as follows:

---

<margin>Gauri is Parvati; the <term>Ketaka</term> leaf is situated in her ear; the flower of the <term>Ketaka</term> is like a gold fragment on the mountain.</margin>
<margin>Therefore, because of that, the Master's two sons... the second tooth... auspicious... anger.</margin>

# ॥ Salutations to the Illustrious <term>Ganesha</term> ॥ Salutations to the Illustrious Sun-god ॥
<gloss>The meter is Indravajra</gloss> <gloss>Moving quickly</gloss> <gloss>Establishing with the trunk</gloss> <gloss>On the small cheek</gloss>

Having pulled the shred of a <term>Ketaka</term> leaf from <term>Gauri’s</term> ear, he placed it before his mouth with his hand. May the elephant-faced god [<term>Ganesha</term>], whose second tusk was fashioned in a <term>Muhurta</term> <note>original: "muhurtta-kalita-dvitiya-danta"; a poetic reference to Ganesha's single tusk</note>, destroy all obstacles. ॥ 1 ॥

<gloss>The Serpent-faced one</gloss> <gloss>Ganesha</gloss> <gloss>The meter is Upajati</gloss>

Rama, the son of <term>Anantadeva</term>, extends the <term>Muhurtachintamani</term> ("The Thought-Gem of Auspicious Moments"), which is the cause for understanding the collection of rituals and which contains the essence of the cosmic play. ॥ 2 ॥

<gloss>Expansion</gloss> <gloss>The light of infinite knowledge</gloss> <gloss>Well-known</gloss> <gloss>He describes</gloss> <gloss>These are the lords of the lunar days, beginning with the strength of Fire</gloss>

<gloss>The purification of the lunar day</gloss>
<gloss>The Serpent</gloss> <gloss>The Twin-gods</gloss> <gloss>The purpose of the seven days from the first day of the Sun</gloss>

<margin>In this manner, with their divisions, those lunar days are auspicious, middling, or inauspicious. In this [astrological calculation], the results are auspicious, middling, or inauspicious. Thus, the lunar days are joined with the days of the week... just as on Sunday, the <term>Bharani</term> star... on Monday, the <term>Chitra</term> star... in order, <term>Bhadra</term> is second, <term>Manda</term> is reversed...</margin>

[The presiding deities of the lunar days are:] Fire, <term>Ganesha</term>, <term>Guha</term> (Skanda), the Sun, <term>Shiva</term>, <term>Durga</term>, the Serpent, the Universal Gods, <term>Hari</term> (Vishnu), the God of Love, <term>Shiva</term>, and the Moon. ॥ 3 ॥

The <term>Tithis</term> (lunar days) are named <term>Nanda</term>, <term>Bhadra</term>, <term>Jaya</term>, <term>Rikta</term>, and <term>Purna</term>. They are respectively auspicious, middling, and forbidden. In the bright fortnight, they are excellent, middling, and inferior. During the bright fortnight, when the Moon is in the house of Mars or Saturn...

<gloss>At the end of the Sun's transit</gloss> <gloss>They give middling results</gloss> <gloss>Step by step</gloss>

<margin>Now, the auspicious effects of the lunar day. The application is according to the form of that deity. Now, the names of the lunar days... the name of the main number... which lunar day is powerful on which day of the week... Now, he explains the forbidden combinations... as on the eighth day, one should not consume meat... on the fourteenth day, honey... on the New Moon, one should not engage in [sexual] service.</margin>

...are successful. ॥ 4 ॥ <term>Nanda</term>, <term>Bhadra</term>, <term>Jaya</term>, <term>Rikta</term>, and <term>Purna</term> are the names. These are successful when combined with the Sun, the stars of <term>Tvashtri</term> (Chitra), the <term>Vishvedevas</term> (Uttarashadha), and...

<gloss>The star Uttaraphalguni</gloss> <gloss>The star Revati</gloss> <gloss>Saturday</gloss> <gloss>The 'Samvartaka' yoga</gloss>

॥ 5 ॥ On a Sunday, if the Moon is in <term>Jyeshtha</term>, the end of the transit, or <term>Vishakha</term>, it is successful. ॥ 5 ॥ Afterward, the lunar days [are classified]: <term>Manda</term> (slow), the second, the first day, and on Wednesday, the seventh is inferior. The sixth day and others, when the Sun is...

<gloss>They are forbidden; the sixth</gloss> <gloss>The first day and the Shravana star</gloss> <gloss>In the removal of wood, etc.</gloss> <gloss>The person</gloss> <gloss>The hour</gloss> <gloss>Sexual intercourse</gloss>

...washing the teeth. ॥ 6 ॥ On the sixth, eighth, fourteenth, and the day of the Moon’s waning, one should not consume: oil, meat, sugarcane, or red [foods]. One should not engage in: sexual intercourse, or oil massage. ॥ 12 ॥ On the tenth, ninth, or second lunar day, bathing with <term>Amala</term> fruit <note>original: "dhatri-phalaih"; Indian gooseberry</note> is [forbidden]... on the seventh, eighth, seventh, twelfth, second, first, and eleventh...

-> (Furthermore, even without faults such as the 'Vapi' or 'Apavadi', the night is auspicious, while the day is inauspicious. Malefic influences are truly present during the nights in auspicious works, and therefore one should always avoid them. ॥ 1 ॥) <-

<summary>This page introduces the Muhurtachintamani with an invocation to Ganesha and a statement of the author Rama’s intent. It begins the astrological technicalities by listing the presiding deities of the lunar days (tithis) and categorizing the days (Nanda, Bhadra, etc.) while listing specific forbidden activities like eating meat or using oil on certain days.</summary>
<keywords>Ganesha, invocation, Rama Daivajña, Tithi (lunar day), Tithi deities, Nanda, Bhadra, Jaya, Rikta, Purna, dietary prohibitions, astrological timing</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining auspicious and inauspicious times (muhurtas). It follows the introductory prayers, moving directly into the calculations for "burned" or "void" lunar days (tithis) and lunar mansions (nakshatras) across various months and weekdays.</meta>

<margin>
Now, concerning the <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions</note> such as <term>Magha</term> and <term>Vishakha</term>, they are not [auspicious] for marriage and other ceremonies; however, for a <term>Sankranti</term> <note>the sun's transit into a new sign</note>, it is necessary [to observe them], as stated in the *Deepika* <note>a classic astrological text</note>. One should avoid the <term>Yamaghanta</term> <note>a specific inauspicious period of the day</note> during rituals like <term>Homa</term> <note>fire offerings</note>... for these specific durations. Here, for the application of <term>Yogas</term> <note>astrological combinations</note>, the period after midnight is considered auspicious. ॥३
</margin>

<gloss>The "Burned" days arise based on the day of the week.</gloss> <gloss>The lunar days starting from the fourth become known as "Poisonous" when occurring on Sundays.</gloss> <gloss>The lunar days starting from the twelfth on Mondays are known as "Fire-consuming" <note>original: "hutashana"</note>. ५</gloss>
Five, Fire <note>original: "agni"; symbolizes 3</note>, Flavors <note>original: "rasa"; symbolizes 6</note>, Eight, Joys <note>original: "nanda"; symbolizes 9</note>, Vedas <note>original: "veda"; symbolizes 4</note>... 10 6 8 9 4 7 6 5 [Mnemonic numbers for lunar days] In the morning... the stainless 7, the 9, the mountains <note>original: "shaila"; symbolizes 7</note>, the suns <note>original: "surya"; symbolizes 12</note>, 12 6 8 7 2 the Ganges 9.

<gloss>Commentary:</gloss>
The 10th and 11th are "Burned" <note>original: "dagdha"</note>; those known as "Knowledge" and "Fire-consuming" are the lunar days occurring on Sunday and other weekdays. <gloss>Mantra:</gloss> <term>Magha</term>, <gloss>Vi.:</gloss> <term>Vishakha</term>, <gloss>Shra.:</gloss> <term>Shravishtha</term>, <gloss>Mu.:</gloss> <term>Mula</term>, <gloss>Kri.:</gloss> <term>Krittika</term>.
<gloss>Dvi.:</gloss> <term>Brahma</term> <note>original: "brahmain"; another name for Rohini</note>, <gloss>Ha.:</gloss> The hands <note>original: "karau"; refers to Hasta nakshatra</note>, <gloss>Ro.:</gloss> These are the <term>Yamaghanta</term> periods. For marriage and other auspicious works, they must be avoided, but for travel, it is essential [to know them]. ॥८ <gloss>For travel:</gloss> <gloss>= In the month of Bhadrapada</gloss>, in <term>Bhadra</term>, <gloss>Tre.:</gloss> on the Moon's day, <gloss>in Ashvina</gloss> 2nd and 12th; in <term>Nabhasya</term> <note>Bhadrapada</note>, in the "Eye of the Reed."

<margin>
Now, for the months starting with <term>Chaitra</term>, the lunar days are declared in the <term>Shardulavikridita</term> and <term>Anushtubh</term> meters = ३
</margin>
<gloss>In Vaishakha:</gloss> 12th and 2nd lunar days. In <term>Madhava</term> <note>Vaishakha</note>; <gloss>in Pausha:</gloss> the 12th. In <term>Pausha</term>, the "Vedas and Arrows" <note>meaning 4 and 5</note>. <gloss>In Ashvina:</gloss> the 6th, 10th, and the 11th of Shiva 20/11. <gloss>In Marghashirsha:</gloss> on the head of the path, the "Two Serpents" <note>meaning 2 and 8</note> in <term>Magha</term>. <gloss>In Chaitra:</gloss> the "Cow and Kola" 8 and 9. <gloss>In the bright fortnight:</gloss> these fall in both fortnights.
<gloss>Passed</gloss> <gloss>Stated</gloss> <gloss>Karttika</gloss>, <gloss>Ashadha</gloss>, <gloss>Phalguna</gloss>, <gloss>Jyeshtha</gloss>, <gloss>Magha</gloss>: <gloss>In these fortnights, these lunar days are "Empty" <note>original: "shunya"</note> in order.</gloss>
These lunar days are declared by the wise for the Virgin <note>Virgo</note> and Mercury. In the months of <term>Urja</term> <note>Karttika</note>, <term>Ashadha</term>, <term>Tapasya</term> <note>Phalguna</note>, and <term>Tapas</term> <note>Magha</note>: 5 8 6 1 9 5. The passions <note>original: "raga"; symbolizes 6</note> 5 6 8 4 10 and the Indras <note>original: "shakra"; symbolizes 14</note>.

: Five 5, five in the bright fortnight, and the nines 9. <insert>Lunar days of the bright fortnight</insert> <gloss>Shra.:</gloss> 5th, 12th, 2nd, 6th in sequence. Thus, for auspiciousness, one should act with... <gloss>done</gloss> <gloss>Ashvini</gloss> on the 12th, <term>Vaishva</term> <note>original: "uttara-ashada"</note>.

<margin>
Now, the faults of the junctions <note>original: "sandhi-dosha"</note> between lunar days and mansions are stated in the <term>Shardula</term> meter = ६
</margin>
<gloss>On the first day:</gloss>
In the beginning, the 11th <term>Anuradha</term>; on the 2nd; on the 5th, the ancestral mansion <gloss>Magha</gloss>; and likewise on the 8th. On the 3rd and 11th, <term>Rohini</term> 12.
<term>Svati</term> and <term>Chitra</term> on the 13th; on the 7th, <term>Hasta</term> and the Rakshasa mansion <note>original: "mula"</note>; on the 9th, <term>Krittika</term>; on the 8th, <term>Purva Bhadrapada</term>; and on the 6th, <term>Rohini</term> 12. <gloss>Rohini-Vishakha</gloss> center.

<margin>
Now, the final <term>Nakshatras</term> for the months starting with <term>Chaitra</term> are stated in the <term>Anushtubh</term> meter. ३
</margin>
<gloss>Ashvini</gloss>, <gloss>Chitra</gloss>, <gloss>Svati</gloss>, <gloss>Uttara Ashadha</gloss>, <gloss>Pushya</gloss>, <gloss>Mrigashira</gloss>, <gloss>Magha</gloss>, <gloss>Jyeshtha</gloss>, <gloss>Ekadashi</gloss>, <gloss>Dhanishtha</gloss>, <gloss>Uttara Ashadha</gloss>, <gloss>Shravana</gloss>, <gloss>Shatabhisha</gloss>, <gloss>Revati</gloss>, <gloss>Purva Bhadrapada</gloss>...
The pillar, the bed, the wind, the universal gods, the portion, the dweller, the junction of the universal ones... these ancestral [mansions] are the junctions of the month.

<margin>
Now, for the months starting with <term>Chaitra</term>, the stars for travelers are stated in <term>Anushtubh</term> = २
</margin>
<gloss>Shra.:</gloss> <gloss>Ashvini</gloss>, <gloss>Hasta</gloss>, <gloss>Shravana</gloss>, <gloss>Mrigashira</gloss>, <gloss>Magha</gloss>, <gloss>Jyeshtha</gloss>. <gloss>These Nakshatras</gloss> <gloss>are the types for wealth [and loss].</gloss>
These stars in the months of Chaitra and others, as explained, bring about the destruction of wealth. ॥५

<summary>This page details various inauspicious astrological conditions, including "Burned" (Dagdha) and "Void" (Shunya) lunar days and mansions, specifically identifying dates to avoid for marriage, travel, and fire rituals. It uses complex mnemonic number systems and poetic meters to categorize these dangerous time-junctions.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, Dagdha Tithi, Shunya Tithi, Nakshatra, Yamaghanta, electional astrology, Chaitra, Vaishakha, inauspicious times</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discourse on astrological timing (Muhurta), transitioning from general rules about "burned" lunar days to specific regional considerations and the influential system of the "Anandadi Yogas." It references the authority of the Varāhasaṃhitā (the Great Compilation of Varāhamihira) and the sage Narada.</meta>

# In the Middle Country, as stated in the *Varāhasaṃhitā* <note>original: "Varāhasaṃhitāyam" — a foundational 6th-century text on omens and geography</note>: The regions of Bhadraswa, Maru, Mandavya, Salva, Nipa, and Ujjahana; Maru-vatsa, Ghosha, the Yamuna and Saraswati rivers, Matsya, and Madhyamika; Mathura and the Jyotisha forest regions...

# Also the Rasena, Gauda <note>modern Bengal</note>, Nipa, Uddehika, and the Five Panchalas; Saketa, Kuru, Kuru-koti, Kuru-jangala, and the Pariyatra mountains; the Audumbara, Kapila, Gajarvaya, and Shveta—this is known as the "Middle Country." ॥ २

<margin>
Now, concerning the conjunction of the <term>Tithis</term> <note>lunar days</note> with <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions</note> such as <term>Hasta</term> and others. The sage Narada (5) explains why certain combinations are condemned.
</margin>

<gloss>The 3rd,</gloss> <gloss>the 5th,</gloss> <gloss>the 7th,</gloss> <gloss>the 9th,</gloss> <gloss>the 11th,</gloss> <gloss>and the 13th [lunar days]</gloss>
...when the <term>Lagna</term> <note>rising sign or ascendant</note> is Aries, Virgo, Libra, Sagittarius, Cancer, Pisces, Capricorn, or Leo, these are generally considered to bestow faults <note>original: "dosha"; inauspicious influences</note>. ॥ १६ From the start of the lunar fortnight, the odd-numbered lunar days... <margin><gloss>On the first lunar day,</gloss> the odd-numbered lunar days are like "Burned Ascendants" <note>original: "dagdha-lagna"</note>, as the teacher says. Starting from the bright fortnight, the odd lunar days 2, 7, 12...</margin>
...Leo and Capricorn, Gemini, Sagittarius, Aries, Scorpio, Cancer, and Leo. <gloss>In the evening,</gloss>
According to Narada: "The lunar days and the months... those known as 'Void Ascendants' <note>original: "shunya-lagna"</note> should be avoided in the Middle Country, as they bring no fruit to men." १८ <gloss>These should be avoided for marriage and the first feeding of a child.</gloss> The "Lame, Blind, and One-eyed" Ascendants <note>original: "pangu-andha-kāṇa"; technical terms for specific astronomical defects in a rising sign</note> and the "Void Signs" of the months should be particularly abandoned in the Gauda and Malava regions; in other countries, they are not as strictly prohibited. १९ "One should avoid all important works on the 5th lunar day when the moon is in <term>Hasta</term>," "Mars in <term>Ashwini</term> on the 7th," or "the Moon in <term>Ardra</term> on the 6th." २० <gloss>Mars, Mercury, and the Moon</gloss>
"Mercury in <term>Anuradha</term> on the 8th," "Venus in <term>Revati</term> on the 10th," "Jupiter in <term>Pushya</term> on the 9th," and "Saturn in <term>Rohini</term> on the 11th." २१ "For entering a new house, for travel, and for marriage, respectively: avoid Mars in <term>Ashwini</term> or <term>Rohini</term>, and avoid Saturn in <term>Bharani</term> <note>original: "brahnam"</note> and Jupiter in <term>Pushya</term>." २२ "The series of cycles beginning with Ananda and ending with Kaladanda..." <margin>Now the effects of the Yogas <note>astrological combinations</note> starting with Ananda are told in the Upajati meter: ४</margin>
...[1] Ananda <note>Joy</note>, [2] Kaladanda <note>Staff of Death</note>, [3] Dhuma <note>Smoky</note>, [4] Dhata <note>Creator</note>, [5] Saumya <note>Gentle</note>, [6] Dhvanksha <note>Crow</note>, [7] Ketu <note>Banner</note>, [8] Shrivatsa, [9] Vajra <note>Lightning</note>, [10] Mudgara <note>Hammer</note>, [11] Sarvamitra <note>Friend of All</note>, [13] Manasa <note>Mental</note>, [17] Padma <note>Lotus</note>... [16] Utpata <note>Calamity</note>, [17] Mrityu <note>Death</note>, [18] Kana-kala <note>Time's Particle</note>, [19] Siddhi <note>Success</note>, [20] Shubha <note>Auspicious</note>, [21] Amrita <note>Nectar</note>, [22] Musala <note>Pestle</note>, [23] Gada <note>Mace</note>, [24] Matanga <note>Elephant</note>, [25] Rakshasa <note>Demon</note>, [26] Chara <note>Moving</note>, [27] Susthira <note>Very Steady</note>, [28] Pravardhamana <note>Increasing</note>. Their fruits are according to their names. २९ <gloss>Ashwini, Hasta, Sun...</gloss>

<summary>This page lists the geographical boundaries of the "Middle Country" (Central India) and provides specific astrological warnings regarding inauspicious planetary and lunar combinations (Yogas). It highlights that certain "Void" or "Defective" ascendants are particularly dangerous in the Gauda and Malava regions for ceremonies like marriage or travel.</summary>
<keywords>Varahasamhita, Madhya-desha, Gauda, Malava, Tithi, Lagna, Nakshatra, Anandadi Yoga, auspicious timing, Narada, technical astrology</keywords>

Regarding the lunar days <term>Tithis; days based on the moon's phase</term>, from the midpoint of the second watch <term>Prahara; a three-hour division of the day</term> onwards, the brave and others <note>This likely refers to different classes of people or activities</note> should calculate. Just as these are situated in five watches, so too from the sign of Virgo onwards: on the eleventh day <note>Ekadashi</note>, it is in the third watch; on the second day <note>Dvitiya</note>, in the fourth watch; on the seventh day <note>Saptami</note>, in the fifth watch; on the tenth day <note>Dashami</note>, in the third watch; and on the fourteenth day <note>Chaturdashi</note> of the dark fortnight, it begins in the first watch. In these impure states of the lunar days, excluding certain portions, one watch remains. There are three such periods known as "five-fold" where auspicious work can be done; likewise, for those spanning four watches, there is a period known as the "fear of seven ghatikas." This should be observed elsewhere too. Regarding the positions in the watches, those in the west and those in the center—the measure of the day becomes one-sixteenth part. Likewise, because of this western influence, these are placed on the banks, on both shores, and in the region of Nishukara.

->**Now, the Purpose of the Planetary Hour**<-

Now, regarding the effects of the planetary hours during the workday. If there is a flaw <note>an inauspicious astrological influence</note> inherent to the day itself, it is said here that one may still perform primary actions—such as **wearing new clothes** and the like—during the appropriate planetary hour <term>Kala Hora; a division of the day roughly equal to an hour, ruled by a specific planet</term>. This is especially necessary if the task cannot be delayed. <gloss>For example, on a Monday, at 6 hours and 21 minutes, the remaining time is 48... divided by seven, the remainder is 4... this is how the planetary hour of the Sun is calculated. If the desired time falls during the day, then the hour belongs to Mercury; if on a Saturday, then the hour belongs to Mars. Thus, the timing for mantras and other rituals should be understood.</gloss>

<margin>The transit into Capricorn and Cancer always marks the northern course and southern course respectively. 3</margin>

---

->The holy time is sixteen ghatikas on both sides. This is the meaning: if the transit occurs during the day-and-night cycle, the latter half of the day is always holy. If the transit occurs after the day-and-night cycle, then only the first half of the next day is holy. 1<-

<summary>This page concludes the rules for consecrating sacrificial halls and deities based on lunar mansions and begins the chapter detailing the "Punya-kala" (holy time) and social effects of the Sun's transit (Sankranti) through various zodiac signs.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Solar Transits (Sankranti) initiated at the end of the previous page. It transitions from general rules about timing to specific predictions regarding economic stability and symbolic "vehicles" of the transit.</meta>

Now, regarding the interval between sunrise and sunset: if the Sun’s transit <note>Sankranti: the moment the Sun moves from one zodiac sign to the next</note> occurs during that interval, the two days surrounding it are considered auspicious. This means: if the transit occurs before sunrise, that day is holy; if it occurs exactly at the time of sunrise, the following day is holy. Likewise, for the Capricorn Transit <note>Makara Sankranti, marking the winter solstice</note>, the auspicious time <note>Punya-kala</note> falls only on the following day, not the preceding one. = 1

Now, sixteen *ghatikas* <note>A ghatika is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes; 16 ghatikas is roughly 6 hours and 24 minutes</note> are assigned as the holy period. Following the manual’s instructions, we now describe the Equinoxes and the "Eighty-Six" transits <note>Shadashiti: specific transits into dual-natured signs like Gemini or Virgo</note>. Sixteen *ghatikas* provide merit, though not always equally. Whichever transit occurs, it brings much merit. = 2

5 If the transit occurs at midnight, both days are auspicious. This applies whether it occurs before or after the sunrise/sunset markers. 6 The twilight period is measured as three *nadis* <note>synonym for ghatika</note> from the appearance of the solar disk. These transits—southern and northern—occur in order. 7 <margin>|| On the Transit of Cancer || Ashadha 10 ||</margin> <gloss>Or the Libra-Aries transits</gloss> At the southern course and the Equinox <gloss>The first 15 ghatikas</gloss> beginning with <gloss>Libra</gloss> and the middle of <gloss>Aries</gloss>. For Libra and Aries <gloss>it is half.</gloss> <gloss>These are indeed the halves.</gloss> In the "Eighty-Six" transits and the northern course, the latter <gloss>seven (10?) ghatikas in the transits</gloss> <gloss>but in the Capricorn transit</gloss> <gloss>the first 30 ghatikas.</gloss> <margin>The middle is sixteen ghatikas on both sides of the moment of transit. What follows is as before = 2</margin>
These are very auspicious. 8 Likewise, the degrees of the transit <gloss>when an Equinox transit occurs</gloss> are twenty <gloss>20</gloss> 6 <gloss>passed</gloss> and multiplied <gloss>this results in the number seventy.</gloss> Calculated by one's own method, from Aries onwards, the movement... 
<margin>The Sun's position... 30 above 7. For Cancer 30... the twilight time is evening and sunrise... the solar disk is two ghatikas for morning twilight and three ghatikas for evening twilight. In that context, for the morning and evening twilights of the southern and northern courses, both the preceding and following days are auspicious. If the transit occurs during the evening twilight, the previous day is the holy time, not the following day. If the Capricorn transit occurs without crossing the evening twilight, the following day is holy, not the previous. Similarly, for the Cancer transit, if it occurs before the evening twilight, that is the holy time, not after. Regarding the "Star" <note>tara: a system of calculating daily luck based on the moon's position relative to one's birth</note>: On the day the star's influence is to be determined, one should count from the birth star to the star of the current day. Count up to twenty; if the birth star day has passed, the total count should be divided by nine. The remainder determines the "Star" influence. From that, one must understand whether the star is auspicious or inauspicious. <meta>This page continues the technical astrological instructions for the Upanayana (Sacred Thread) ceremony. <margin>
In any state of purity, if this [ceremony] is performed during the three watches of the night, one should be devoted to the knowledge. However, if Rahu <note>The north node of the moon, associated with eclipses</note> is present, it is an obstacle... 15. If the Moon is in its own portion <term>Svansha: a specific subdivision of a zodiac sign owned by the same planet</term> during the initiation, the child will become wealthy. = 3
If Venus, Jupiter, or the Moon are in their own signs but joined with the Sun, the initiate will be without virtues. If joined with Mars, he will be cruel in his actions. If a malefic planet is in the ascendant, he may be of poor character. If the planets are favorable, he will be compassionate. If Venus, Jupiter, or the Moon are each in their own divisions and well-placed, he will be clever and an expert in all sciences. = 4
</margin>

<margin>
Forbidden study days <term>Anadhyaya: days when the chanting of the Vedas is paused</term> are of two types: regular and occasional. Among them, the regular forbidden days are: from the 10th of the bright fortnight to the 2nd of the dark fortnight, the 11th of the bright fortnight of Magha <note>mid-January to mid-February</note>, and the 3rd of the bright fortnight. These are forbidden days. Also, the two-day periods of the 14th, the full moon, the new moon, and the 1st day of the lunar month. The twilight hours are also included. = 5
</margin>

Like the 13th day and others, the three-day period starting from the 7th, and the 4th day are specifically mentioned. These eight are called the "Gala-grahas" <note>literally "throat-seizers," specific lunar days when the study of the Vedas is restricted because it is believed the knowledge will not be retained</note>. 1
Sacrificing, officiating at sacrifices, giving gifts, receiving gifts, teaching, and studying—the twice-born <note>Brahmin</note> who performs these six duties shares in the merit of the ritual. **1**

Regarding the Brahmin, the age for the sacred thread is mentioned; for the Kshatriya and Vaishya, it is the 22nd or 24th year. ## Now begins the Chapter on Marriage. ||

<margin>
Regarding the bride's side, the prescribed rites must be performed to avoid the combination for widowhood. One must investigate the yoga <note>planetary alignment</note> that causes widowhood and use remedies like the "Jatakarsha" or other rituals to prevent it. By considering the eighth house and the signs of widowhood, and by performing the appropriate atonements, the evil effects are neutralized. If a woman is destined for widowhood due to the seventh house placement, she should first be married to an image of Vishnu or a clay pot <note>a practice called Kumbha-vivaha to "absorb" the bad luck</note> by the father or guardians. This ensures the maiden's long life and removes the blemish of future widowhood.
</margin>

<meta>This page continues the transition from the previous discussion on the homecoming of a student to the specific astrological and social requirements for choosing a bride and timing a wedding. It details the ritual of 'Kanyavarana' (selecting the bride) and provides complex astrological rules regarding the months, the age of the couple, and the avoidance of certain periods for firstborn children.</meta>

<margin>
१६
<term>Kanyavarana Muhurta: The auspicious time for selecting a bride</term>
First, after observing the girl, the selection should be considered. On a day before the wedding, the observation of the girl should be done. When the time is determined to be auspicious, and the omens are favorable—such as the appearance of sweet-natured women or people who are satisfied—one should perform the bride-selection <note>original: "kanya-varanam"</note> by offering fruits and flowers to ensure mutual satisfaction. This is the established rule. = ३
</margin>

<margin>
"Son and daughter," "daughter and son," or "two sons"—these terms follow the rule of <term>Ekashesha: a grammatical rule where one word remains to represent a pair or group</term>. Specifically, the masculine form "sons" can include daughters in a general sense. In the same way, the wife is half of the husband's body. Therefore, sons and daughters born from the same mother <note>original: "eka-matra-jah"</note> should be treated with equal honor and regard in all rituals. = ५
</margin>

<gloss>the girl</gloss>
## One should give her to a long-lived groom, for then the fault of a second marriage <note>original: "punarbhavah"; implying widowhood and remarriage</note> will not occur. ७ When the <term>Lagna: the rising sign or ascendant</term> is in Libra <note>original: "Tula"</note>, and the moment is favorable, a companionable and loving wife is found. 
## At that moment, whether the child to be born is a girl or a boy, the wise declare that such a spouse is destined for them. <gloss>it is said</gloss> If the sound of a conch or a drum is heard, or if heroes or auspicious signs appear, it is very favorable. 
## One should also observe if a crow, a donkey, a dog, or even a jackal makes a sound at the time of the query <note>original: "prashna-lagna"</note>. ८ <gloss>regarding the query</gloss> In the presence of Vishnu <note>perhaps referring to a temple or an image</note>, or in a former residence, one should offer garments, ornaments, fruits, and flowers. By satisfying them, the selection of the bride <note>original: "kanya-varam"</note> becomes successful. ९ <gloss>The Earth-gods (Brahmins) or...</gloss>
## The brother of the bride, on an auspicious day, accompanied by songs and musical instruments, should formalize the groom's status by giving him garments and a sacred thread <note>original: "yajnopavita"</note>. This should be done under stable constellations <note>original: "dhruva-yutair"</note>. 
## १० <gloss>The Marriage</gloss> For the sake of Jupiter, Venus, and the Brahmins, the marriage of girls should ideally happen in <gloss>Even Years</gloss> even-numbered years of age (such as the 6th, 8th, or 10th). Beyond the tenth year, the purity of the Sun's position must be checked for the girl, and the purity of Indra's <note>referring to a specific astrological calculation</note> for the groom. ११ 
## Marriage is auspicious in the signs of Gemini, Aquarius, Capricorn, and Scorpio. It is also considered pure when the Sun is in Gemini. The "taking of the hand" <note>original: "kara-pidanam"; a synonym for marriage</note> may also take place in the months of Kartika, Pausha, and Madhu <note>the month of Chaitra</note>. १२ 
## <gloss>Regarding the firstborn son and daughter</gloss> For a son or daughter who is the firstborn of the womb <note>original: "adya-garbha"</note>, <gloss>marriage</gloss> during their birth month, on their birth date, or under their birth star is not praised by the wise. However, it is acceptable for those born second. १३ 
## The month of Jyeshtha <note>May/June</note> is considered "excellent" for some, "middling" for others, and "victorious" for others, yet some say it is not suitable at all. Some say the "triple Jyeshtha" <note>the eldest son, the eldest daughter, and the month of Jyeshtha</note> should be avoided. The end of the month is never recommended for marriage. १४ 
## <gloss>Regarding the marriage of a son</gloss> Within six months of a son's marriage, one should not perform the marriage of a daughter in the same family, nor should a tonsure ceremony <note>original: "mundana"</note> be performed. <gloss>For siblings</gloss> For those born of the same mother, <gloss>The marriage of siblings = should be done within six months</gloss> <gloss>The marriage of a sibling's daughter = should be done within six months</gloss> <gloss>marriage = should be done within six months</gloss> otherwise it is not auspicious. १५ <gloss>For the girl and the groom</gloss> A <gloss>wise person</gloss> knows that if this is ignored, the prosperity of the lineage of both the bride and the groom will be destroyed.

<insert>
This means that if a girl is the eldest <note>original: "jyeshtha"</note>, her marriage in the month of Jyeshtha is not fitting according to the local customs. The marriage of two firstborns <note>a Jyeshtha groom and a Jyeshtha bride</note> should never be performed in the month of Jyeshtha. If one is the eldest and the other is not, it is considered "middling." If neither is the eldest, marriage in the month of Jyeshtha is auspicious. Some say that in an emergency, even if the groom and bride are both firstborns, the marriage in Jyeshtha is acceptable, but others disagree. To avoid any obstacles or evil, one must carefully observe these rules for a successful and happy union. = ३

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of marriage compatibility (Kuta) in Hindu astrology. It specifically addresses exceptions to the "bad matches" (Shashtashtaka and Dwi-dwadasha) where the friendship between the ruling planets of the bride's and groom's zodiac signs can neutralize harmful influences.</meta>

Just as in the case of the signs Aries and Scorpio, where Rahu and Sagittarius represent the ninth house relationship, and the relationship between the two lords is the second and twelfth <note>original: "dwivardshamam"; referring to the 'Dwi-dwadasha' or 2-12 house positioning</note>, one should still act. For instance, if there is mutual friendship <note>original: "sohride"</note> between the lords of the signs or the masters of the zodiac signs, the fault of the "pulse" <term>nadi-dosha: a biological or energetic mismatch believed to affect the health of the couple or their children</term> does not occur. Even if there is a "bad match" <term>dushta-kuta: an unfavorable score in the compatibility system</term>, the marriage is auspicious if the lords are friends. 

As an example, the sixth-eighth relationship <term>shashtashtaka: a configuration where the bride’s and groom’s signs are six and eight places apart, usually considered very inauspicious</term> between the signs of Pisces and Leo should not be avoided; similarly, the second-twelfth relationship between Pisces and Aries, and so on. In these sixth-eighth cases, if the lords of the other signs are mutual friends or even neutral, and there is strength in the lord of the lineage <note>original: "vamsha-svamin"</note>, and there is no affliction of the "pulse" or lunar mansion <note>original: "nadi nakshatra"</note>, and if there is purity of the guiding star <note>original: "tara-shuddhi"</note> and mutual attraction <note>original: "vashyatve"</note>, then even in a "bad match" the marriage is declared auspicious and beautiful by the sages.

Similarly, the fault of the ninth-part joined with a malefic or the Moon is destroyed (90). Jupiter <gloss>doubles</gloss> this destruction. "When the Lord of the ascendant is in a quadrant or the 11th house, he pacifies the collection of faults just as fire consumes a heap of cotton" (92).

<margin>
Now, the method for calculating the portions of the signs <note>original: "Amsha"</note> and the rising times. The rising of the signs at one's own location <note>original: "Svadeshiya Udaya"</note> is determined from the end of the previous sign. The rising times for Aries and the other signs are measured in *Palas* <note>a unit of time equal to 24 seconds</note>. By multiplying these and dividing by twenty, the elapsed portions are found. Thus, the rising of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> and its subsequent signs are multiplied by the intended degree. By dividing by twenty, one obtains the results of the degrees already passed. In this way, the total time elapsed in *Palas* is calculated for the Sun and the Ascendant. The difference between the Sun and the Ascendant is added to the rising time. If the value is 26, the tropical portion <note>original: "Sayanansha"</note>, the Ascendant, and the Sun are the factors. The rising at one's own location is then divided by twenty to find the time in *Palas*. 

= 5 By multiplying that, the root of the Ascendant located at a single degree of the tropical position is found from the difference of 90 and 59. After subtracting the intended multiplication, the remainder is 56. The time for the "Tree-Guardian" <note>likely a specific ritual duration</note> is two and six. The rising of the second sign is then calculated for one’s own time. Now, the motion of the planets and their setting must be calculated. After that, the calculations for the various divisions of the zodiac follow. The calculations are subtracted and the proximity is determined. Dividing by seven, one finds the movement. 
</margin>

Through the portions of the signs, the time is calculated in *Ghatikas* <note>24-minute units</note> and *Palas*. The <term>Sankranti</term> <note>the Sun’s transition from one zodiac sign to another</note> occurs at that time. For that specific day, the number of days, *Ghatikas*, and *Palas* are multiplied by the respective planetary motions. From this, the degrees and minutes are obtained. These portions are known by terms such as *Tithi* <note>lunar day</note>. When added to the position already occupied, the result is the clearly defined <term>Bhaskara</term> <note>literally "The Light-Maker," referring here to the calculated position of the Sun or a planet</note> = 2. When calculating the Ascendant, the <term>Navamsha</term> <note>the "ninth-part" or 3°20' division of a sign, used for high-precision astrology</term> is considered. For whichever village or location one is in, the number of *Navamshas* is multiplied by ten. The calculation for the degrees, minutes, and seconds is then performed based on the tropical position.

### Now, the Chapter on Journeys

Regarding journeys, every day 
a king should be given the proper time based on his birth. For those of intelligence, the time is determined by the birth star, the ascendant at the time of the query, and the presence of auspicious omens. If Mercury is favorable, wealth is attained. 
<margin>
Now, the "Which" combination = 4

If the birth time is unknown through the calculation of hours and minutes, the chart for the rising sign at the time of the query should be used. One must investigate the auspicious or inauspicious fruits through the knowledge of the astrologers to provide the king with the day for his journey. If some say the birth time is unknown, then for them, the astrologer should determine the day of departure by looking at the omens, signs, and the rising sign at the time the question is asked. This is the root cause for determining the timing. 6

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for "Yatra" (journeys and military expeditions). For Aries and the other **signs of the zodiac**, one must know the harmful stars <note>original: "ghata-nakshatra"</note>—such as Magha and others. For example: for **Aries**, the star is Magha; for **Taurus**, the fifth; for **Gemini**, the ninth, and so on. Furthermore, when a star shines [in this position], it is like an arrow <note>a symbol of piercing or harm</note>. In marriages, daily activities, and especially in **war**, these harmful factors must be sought out and avoided. In the aforementioned activities, there should be no marriage, no travel, and no auspicious deeds; they must be avoided. ॥

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for "Yatra" (journeys and military expeditions). The previous page detailed the "Ghata" (harmful) factors—specific stars and directional obstacles like the "Iron Bar" (Parigha) that travelers must avoid. This page transitions into the specific auspicious and inauspicious directions based on the movements of Venus (Shukra), the Sun, and the Moon, as well as the planetary lords of the compass points.</meta>

<margin>
When the rays of the Sun <note>original: "Prabhakara"</note> obscure the brilliance of Venus <note>Shukra</note>, one should not begin the first step of a journey. As long as the Moon is not in the path of Venus, the journey may proceed. If Venus is visible in the direction one is traveling, it is "Facing Venus" <note>original: "Shukra-Sammukha"</note>, which is a bad omen. There are three types of this: First, when Venus is directly ahead of the person. Second, when the person leaves their place and Venus is in the North or South, yet effectively remains "in front." Third, when Venus is positioned in constellations like Krittika and others, it is considered "facing" the traveler. One should travel in the direction where Venus is risen and visible; if it is invisible, the outcome must be judged accordingly. ॥ 2 ॥
If the planetary divisions <term>vargas</term> of the birth sign or the current ascendant are strong, victory is certain. Without this strength, even a king is lowered.
</margin>

<margin>
When the divisions <term>varga</term> of the house of the Ascendant are in the center <term>kendra</term> of the chart, the wise approve of travel. Or, if the division of the Ascendant reaches the sixth house, travel in that direction is permitted. Or, when the divisions are in a friendly house, travel is advised. ॥ 1 ॥
Regarding the solstices: when the day and night match the appropriate solstice, travel in that direction. During the Moon's solstice, travel in that direction at night. The Sun is the highest of all. If one acts contrary to these rules, it results in "death" <note>meaning total failure or literal mortality</note>. If the solstice is different from the travel direction... the Sun and Moon...
</margin>

# When the planetary divisions are in the center or on their own day, travel is advised. ॥ 3 ॥

During the **Northern Solstice** <term>Uttarayana</term> <note>the six-month period when the sun moves north</note>, if the Sun and Moon are favorable, one should travel toward the North or the East. During the **Southern Solstice** <term>Dakshinayana</term>, one should travel toward the West. If one travels against these directions during the day or night, or if the solstice is mismatched, destruction <note>original: "vadha", meaning slaughter or ruin</note> will follow. ॥ 38 ॥

One must observe the direction where Venus <note>original: "Go", a poetic term for the planet of light/cattle</note> rises. If Venus is "facing" <note>Sammukha</note> the traveler, they should not go in that direction. ॥ 39 ॥

If a planet is retrograde, eclipsed, or in its sign of debilitation, even a wise king who marches will fall under the control of others. If Mercury or Saturn are the ones moving there, there is no victory; success is never achieved by those who march against such forces. ॥ 40 ॥

As long as Venus is considered "blind" <note>original: "Shukra-andha"; a technical state where Venus loses its influence in certain constellations</note> in the quarters of Taurus and Krittika, it is not harmful. However, a king should halt in the middle of the path if Venus is facing him. ॥ 41 ॥

The sign of **Aquarius** <note>Kumbha</note> and the sub-division of Aquarius must be avoided at all costs by the wise. For a king traveling then, his chariots will be destroyed at every step. ॥ 42 ॥

If the Ascendant at birth and the Lord of the birth sign are auspicious planets, then a journey undertaken during their rise is successful. If the travel begins when **Pisces** <note>Meena</note> is the rising sign or its sub-division, the path becomes successful. ॥ 43 ॥

When the birth sign is strong <gloss>strength</gloss> or <gloss>own</gloss> when the lord of the house or the division is in the Ascendant, or if the lords of the Ascendant are present, the king’s intention is fulfilled at every step. ॥ 44 ॥

If the **Moon** or the **Ascendant** is in the "Best Division" <term>Vargottama</term> <note>when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth sub-chart</note>, the journey is said to grant the desired goal. Such a journey is highly praised and provides all success. ॥ 45 ॥

Traveling in the direction of the Ascendant is auspicious; it gives wealth and victory. Conversely, traveling against the Lord of the Direction brings loss, destruction, and fear of enemies. ॥ 46 ॥

The sign <gloss>at birth</gloss> that was associated with auspicious planets at the time of birth, or the sign known as "Vau-sha" <note>a technical classification for victory</note>, brings victory to the king when it rises. Sages have declared this specific combination <term>yoga</term> to be the bestower of victory. ॥ 47 ॥

The Sun, Venus, Mars, Rahu, <gloss>p</gloss> Saturn, <gloss>p</gloss> ... are the lords of the directions. Starting from the East, the lords of the directions and the intermediate corners are assigned as follows: ॥ 48 ॥

When the Lord of the Direction is in the center <term>kendra</term>, the king goes forth and destroys his enemies.
East (18), South-East (19), South (18), South-West (20), West (21), <gloss>North-West</gloss> (22), <gloss>North</gloss> (23), <gloss>North-East</gloss> (24).

<margin>
This combination of rules means that if one does not travel in the direction corresponding to the Moon's solstice during the day, the traveler will face death. ॥ 4 ॥
When planets are retrograde, eclipsed, or debilitated, the King will certainly become powerless and fall into the hands of others. If Venus is in that direction, a king of the "Ekadi" clan <note>a specific lineage or classification</note> will not find victory. If one marches while Venus is "facing" <note>Sammukha</note>, there is no victory; victory only comes to those whom Venus follows from behind. Sages say the king is unable to succeed if these rules are ignored. ॥ 5 ॥
The journey is successful if the birth Ascendant or birth sign is rising. ॥ 6 ॥
Regarding the timing of the journey: if one goes when the specific technical combinations <term>yogas</term> for victory are present, the journey grants success. ॥ 7 ॥

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on directional obstacles and the movements of Venus. It shifts from general warnings about "facing" Venus to specific planetary configurations—known as Yogas—that guarantee success in travel and military expeditions. # As it is for the body and the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, so is it for the departure; the stars determine the journey || In the case of men, the Son of Bhrigu <note>original: "Bhrigu-suto"; refers to the planet Venus</note> in the eleventh or twelfth house makes for a successful journey. # Chapter Nineteen

<warning>Text bleed-through from the reverse page; dense marginalia on the right and left margins.</warning>

<margin>Now, the auspicious timing <term>Muhurta: a specific calculated moment in time</term> for Victory Tenth <note>original: "Vijayadashami," a festival celebrating the victory of good over evil, traditionally auspicious for starting military campaigns</note> is described in the Totaka meter:</margin>

If Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> is in the Ascendant <term>Tanu: the first house of a horoscope, representing the self</term>, <gloss>this is one alignment</gloss> or if Jupiter, Venus <note>original: "Sita"</note>, and the Moon <note>original: "Bhanu-vaha"</note> are placed in the signs of the water element in a beneficial lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra: a star cluster or "lunar house"</term>... 74 <gloss>This involves the third house; the wise say this brings clarity and health. If Mercury, Venus, or Jupiter are in the center houses, it forms a specific alignment. If these occur during a certain phase of the moon, it is called a "Superior Alignment" <note>original: "Yogadhiyoga"</note>.</gloss>

When one planet is so placed, it is a "Yoga"; when two or more are so placed, it is a "Superior Alignment." Such an alignment is remembered as the "Complete Superior Alignment." <gloss>This alignment brings prosperity, security, and the binding or defeat of enemies.</gloss> Even a man without power obtains gold-like wealth by attaining this Superior Alignment. 75

The Tenth day of the bright half of the month, when paired with the star Shravana, is called "The Victorious" <note>original: "Vijaya"</note>. It is said to bring success in all auspicious deeds. When joined with Shravana, it is especially a giver of blessings; for a King’s departure, it ensures the attainment of victory. 76

<margin>Rites such as the sacred thread ceremony <term>Vratabandha</term>, the consecration of a deity, and festivals like Holi may be affected by ritual impurity <term>Sutaka: impurity following a birth or death in the family</term>. According to the scriptures, these should not be completed if the period of impurity has not ended.</margin>

Knowing the strength of the omens <term>Shakuna: signs or bird-omens</term> and the planetary strength, the Lord of the People <gloss>The King</gloss> sets out and attains success. One should act according to these omens, for mental liberation and success are greater when one acts in accordance with them. 77

The sacred thread ceremony, the consecration of a deity, marriage <note>original: "Kara-pida," literally "grasping the hand"</note>, and festivals should not be performed during times of ritual impurity. <gloss>One should move</gloss> only when the time is pure, even if it is during a year of heavy rain. 78

When a King's departure and re-entry into the city occur on an inauspicious day, <gloss>then</gloss> the wise person should never even consider the "Yogini" <term>Yogini: a directional energy or goddess whose location determines if travel is safe</term> to be favorable. 79

Peace-making rituals <note>original: "Shanti"</note> should be done first before a departure. One should never make an entry on the ninth lunar day, nor under certain stars or on certain weekdays. 80

If a King’s departure and entry happen on the same day, the wise should carefully consider which time is for the entry. 81

After offering to the fire <term>Homa</term>, worshipping the deity, bowing to and honoring the Priests <note>original: "Vipras"</note>, and meditating on the Lord of the Directions, the King should give gifts to children and then set out. 82

<margin>Another reading exists regarding the nature of the dwelling or the grain.</margin> <margin>Now follows the "Tortoise Chart" <term>Kurma Chakra: a map of the earth or a site projected onto a tortoise shape for divination</term> for the sacred thread ceremony, involving meat, birds, and the earth... offerings of clarified butter and wine... fish is mentioned... barley... for the sake of victory...</margin>

One should offer boiled grains <term>Kulmasha: a type of sour gruel or boiled pulse</term>, sesame, and rice, as well as <gloss>sweets and pancakes</gloss>, meat <gloss>of the deer, goat, or wild animals</gloss>, milk, and wine. 83

Beans <gloss>of the Masha variety</gloss>, grain, and <gloss>millet</gloss>, honey, or various goats... all of these. 84

In the Tortoise Chart, <gloss>at various points</gloss>, one should offer meat, wine, or kidney beans, as well as millet, barley, and fish. 85

In the twenty-seven stars, one should consider the offerings of boiled grains and other foods. Whether through different types of fish or other preparations, one who acts with this knowledge... <note>The text becomes fragmented here, discussing the ritual mixing of sesame and rice.</note>

# Rituals for Departure and Success

<warning>The manuscript features dense handwriting with numerous marginal and interlinear annotations. Some ink bleeding from the reverse side is visible.</warning>

Even if one has committed a great sin like the killing of a priest <note>original: "Brahma-hatya"</note> or other grave wrongs, by following these rules, one avoids suffering and attains success and firm happiness. If a task is undertaken without delay or hesitation, and with the proper placement of offerings <note>original: "Nikshepa"</note>, these obstacles are removed, just as meat is avoided or weapons are set aside in specific rituals.
<gloss>Commentary: In an auspicious moment, the gods grant pure results for the army. Whether it is a worldly task or a divine one, the mind should be steady like the wind and focused on the deity.</gloss>

Indeed, twenty-four types of "painted rice" <note>rice colored with saffron or turmeric</note>, honey, and Varuni liquor are combined; a wise man, considering the difficulty of the task, should observe this with devotion. 74

<margin>

27
28

When the King rises to depart, he should first place his right foot forward. 
He should look upon a Priest <note>original: "Vipra"</note>, gold, and a full jar.
The king should then proceed while looking at the celestial bodies. 
At the time of mounting his horse or elephant, he should hold a copper vessel containing gold.
First, he should give to the Astrologer <note>original: "Ganaka"</note> to remove defects.
Depending on the direction, he should follow the prescribed path for the sake of bliss and lightness.
Now, concerning the Temple <note>original: "Deva-griha"</note> or the residence: 
The King's bow and arrows should be kept at a distance. 
Having stood there, he proceeds. 
Even if he has already departed, a wise King should still move with great effort according to the experts in domestic rituals. 
On that current day, no other work should be done. 83 
</margin>

Who would not seek a home for peace?

This is the "Dohada" for the directions. 
In this way, one by one, the directions are honored.

The journey should be undertaken with great force; as far as the King goes, that is the extent of the departure, according to Bharadvaja. Now, the sage Vashistha describes the end of the departure and the heart of the journey. 5

Without obstacles, if the ground is level, one should not stay more than one night in between. 84 On a journey, one should not stay for five nights, but if one stays for a "Varuna" period <note>possibly related to the West or water</note>, then seven nights. 6 After that, having crossed the prescribed days, one should again turn the face toward the destination and proceed after considering the astrological timing. 85

<margin>
Now the rules for departure are stated: One should not engage in sexual intercourse <note>original: "maithuna"</note> on the first day of the month or during the transition of seasons. If one is unable to restrain oneself, then at least on the day of departure, intercourse must be avoided. 1 On fasting days or when the moon is in a certain position, one should behave as a sage. If one encounters fish, meat, or human beings as they leave, it is a sign of success. As long as the omens remain favorable, there is no fault in the timing. If five specific signs are seen, success is certain. One should only enjoy pleasures after conquering enemies.
</margin>

One should not engage in intercourse on days that are not considered auspicious, or on the day of departure itself, even if the mind is set upon it. 84 After consuming milk, one must abstain for three nights. One should recite prayers during the night. On the previous day, one should avoid oil and certain foods. <gloss>during the whole month</gloss> The king should not abandon his protection of the people. 85 If one avoids oil and jaggery on Sundays <gloss>oil</gloss> <gloss>on Sunday</gloss>, then for those months, illness will retreat from that person. <gloss>recovery from disease</gloss> One should honor women, priests <note>original: "dvija"</note>, and the elderly. 86 If during the four months of the rainy season one observes fasts, even if they encounter fish or meat, as long as it is placed on the earth, there is no fault. 87 If there is a small fault, there is no need for fear; however, if there is a great roar of clouds or a wolf is heard, <gloss>this is an excessive sign</gloss> <gloss>of fire</gloss> then one should look toward the sun. After feeding a priest, one may proceed. One should abandon oil and its associations. 88 <gloss>The peacock and the blue jay are the birds meant here</gloss> Seeing a priest eating, curd, a cow, <gloss>eating</gloss> a bird, a courtesan, or hearing musical instruments <gloss>eating</gloss> <gloss>the rooster, the merchant, the bird, the singer, and the dancer</gloss> are favorable.

A peacock, a blue jay, or a potter <gloss>if the potter gives liquor, etc., otherwise not</gloss> <gloss>a physician</gloss>, carrying vessels, turmeric, or a girl of good character carrying gems, pearls, or holy ash—these are excellent signs. 99 <gloss>a married couple</gloss> <gloss>saffron</gloss> <gloss>a washerman with bright clothes</gloss> A mirror, a treasure, a washerman, fish, a royal throne, or even a corpse provided there is no wailing <note>A "silent corpse" was sometimes interpreted as a sign that obstacles have been "laid to rest."</note> <gloss>the wife</gloss> <gloss>delight on a flower-bed, the priority of the Vedas</gloss>, a banner, wine, an elephant, a horse, "gorochana" <note>a bright yellow pigment from a cow, used for tilaks</note>, a sparrow, and a full water jar following behind are all signs of success at the time of departure. 100 <gloss>an ascetic</gloss> Conversely, seeing a barren woman, a bone, a snake, salt, ashes, smoke, oil, an intoxicated person, or a person with matted hair <gloss>is like seeing a victorious enemy</gloss>, a monk, someone naked or covered in oil, someone with disheveled hair, a fallen person, a person with a missing limb, one afflicted by hunger, a wicked woman, or a lizard—these are bad omens if seen while leaving. 101 Seeing someone in saffron robes, jaggery, buttermilk, mud, a widow, a hunchback, or witnessing a quarrel in the family, seeing torn clothes, or a spider... <gloss>clothes from a birth</gloss> <gloss>five</gloss> grains, cotton, vomiting, or the sound of a donkey are signs that block the path. A shaven-headed person, a deaf person, or a person with a limp are not auspicious.

<meta>This page continues the transition from the previous discussion on travel omens into the principles of Vastu Shastra (traditional Indian architecture). Multiply the letters of the village name by six, then divide by the qualities <note>original: "guna", representing the number 3</note>; if the remainder is favorable, the village shall be auspicious by name. Doubling one's own class and dividing by the other class, the remainder represents the *kakini* <term>kakini: a traditional unit of measurement or value used here to calculate the energetic weight of a location</term>. Of these two, the one that is greater is preferred. Thus, one should evaluate the house and the enemy <note>original: "ari"</note> to ensure the entrance and prosperity are not harmed in any way. **1** For those born under the signs of the Cow (Taurus), the Lion (Leo), or the Crocodile (Capricorn), a union with the village is beneficial. Within the center of the village, the eastern direction is not for counting; the signs of Cancer and Pisces do not bring profit. Just as horses are strong in their own fifth position, so too are these. **2**

When the width is multiplied by the length and divided by a specific factor, the remainder determines the "Income" <term>aya: one of the eight astrological formulas used to determine the spatial orientation and proportion of a building</term>, such as the Flag <note>original: "dhvaja"</note> and others. For houses, the odd-numbered results are praised; the Flag is auspicious in its own place and in all directions. **1** When the area of the plot is multiplied by eight and divided by the number of constellations <note>original: "bha-sankhya", i.e., 27</note>, the remainder is the "Constellation" of the house. If the house’s constellation is favorable, it brings wealth; if it is the constellation of Varuna (Satabhisha), it brings destruction. **2** One should multiply the dimensions and divide; what remains above and below should be handled by the wise, repeatedly checking the western side. This is the method for determining the Flag and other signs. **3**

**Thus concludes the section in the *Ratnamala* <note>original: "Jewel Necklace", a famous astrological treatise by Shripati</note>.**

When one is subtracted from the length and divided by eight, or when the area is divided by eight, the remainder is the *pinda* <term>pinda: the basic "body" or measurement unit of the building's perimeter</term>. **3**

**Now, the Lamp:**
When the Priest of the Gods (Jupiter) <note>original: "Vidasharaja Guru"</note> resides in the sign of Leo, one must not perform the "grasping of the hand" <note>marriage</note>. If one does so, it will cause the death of the groom. When Jupiter is in the sign of Taurus, the performance of such deeds leads to the destruction of the house for the builder. **1** Tonsure ceremonies <note>original: "chuda"</note>, weddings, the tying of the sacred thread <note>original: "vrata bandha"</note>, initiations, entering a new house <note>original: "grihapravesha"</note>, the installation of deities, pilgrimages to sacred fords, and taking a wife—none of these should be done while the Divine Teacher (Jupiter) is in Leo. **2** As long as the Lord of Speech (Jupiter) stays in the sign of Leo or even in the Leo sub-division <note>original: "simhanshe"</note> of another sign, that time is not for weddings; the remaining time is considered suitable for marriage. **3** If an auspicious month is occurring but a specific "Great Moment" <term>muhurta: a precise auspicious time window</term> cannot be found, one should place a copper plate upon the doorframe at that time. **4** When a truly auspicious moment eventually arrives, one should discard the copper plate and then establish the doorframe and other parts; in doing so, no fault <note>original: "dosha"</note> shall exist. **5**

**Thus ends the dialogue on the Essence of the Lord of Speech from the *Vastu Kutuhala* <note>original: "The Curiosity of Architecture"</note>, a sea of knowledge.**

In the balance of the solar transit, the width and length are multiplied. The Flag is the primary income; Smoke, Lion, Dog, Ox, Donkey, Elephant, and Crow are the others. The *pinda* here is multiplied by eight. **4** The Flag and others face all directions, but the Flag should specifically face the entrance. The Lion should face the South or North. The others—the Fire-sign, the West-sign, and the Fearless-sign—should be placed in the West, North, or East starting from the Flag. **5** Then, the house...

Auspiciousness follows when the planets are well-placed, but a **loss of wealth** occurs when the lords of the center <note>original: "kendri", referring to the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th astrological houses</note> are weak, devoid of strength, or situated in their signs of debilitation. In such a state, the building brings no stability. One should not construct a residence for the learned <note>original: "vibudhavastu"</note> if the site lies directly in front of a temple or a mine, for the movement of energy is blocked there. 

**6** Following the methods of Maya <note>Mayasura, the legendary architect of the asuras</note>, one should calculate the distance and add the names starting with **Dhruva**. When these are combined with the mass <term>pinda: the mathematical product of length, width, and height used in architectural calculations</term>, and divided by the appropriate numerical factors, the results yield the specific characteristics of the house. 

**7** Here are the sixteen types of houses identified by the ancient teachers, beginning with Dhruva: 

1. **Dhruva** (The Constant/Stable) 
2. **Dhanya** (The Prosperous/Grain-filled) **2** 
3. **Jaya** (The Victorious) 
4. **Nanda** (The Joyous) **4** 
5. **Khara** (The Harsh/Sharp) **5** 
6. **Kanta** (The Beloved/Lovely) **6** 
7. **Manorama** (The Mind-Pleasing) **7** 
8. **Sumukha** (The Beautiful-Faced) **8** 
9. **Durmukha** (The Ugly-Faced) **9** 
10. **Crura** (The Cruel/Formidable) **10** 
11. **Ripuda** (The Enemy-Giver) **11** 
12. **Visada** (The Sorrowful) **12** 
13. **Nasha** (The Destructive) **13** 
14. **Akranda** (The Wailing) **14** 
15. **Vipula** (The Abundant) **15** 
16. **Vijaya** (The Triumphant) **16** 

**9** These are the sixteen definitions taught to the learned. For the first, sixth, seventh, and eighteenth configurations, one uses three letters of the name. Digging for the foundation <note>original: "khata"</note> should be performed when the faces of the earth-spirits are turned downward, while the foundation vessel <note>original: "patra"</note> is placed when they face upward. The work is completed successfully when the entry is made under gentle and fixed constellations. **11**

<page-num>29</page-num>

Likewise, in the text **Sajjana-vallabha**, it is said that digging should occur when the 'face' is directed downwards. In the southern and fierce directions, the foundation stone <note>original: "shila"</note> is placed. The placement of the stone for the supreme house should avoid the sides and follow the orientation of the 'face,' so as not to harm the priests.

According to the **Rajavallabha**:
In the left <note>northern</note> portion, one should place the treasury <note>original: "dhana"</note>, the wardrobe, and the house of the gods <note>original: "deva-bhavanam", the shrine room</note>. The place for weapons or horses should be nearby. The dining hall should be located near the garden house. In the southeast <note>the direction of Agni, the fire god</note>, the kitchen should be placed. The armory or place for weapons is also situated here. However, a place for women in the southern direction may lead to decline. The storage for buffaloes and goats, and the cutting of pearls, is auspicious between the south and the southeast. **1**

The sage **Vishwakarma** states:
The sun <note>representing fire or the kitchen</note> should be known to be in the south, while the water-refuge should be on the left <note>north</note>. If this is reversed, it is known as a 'piercing' <note>original: "vaidha", an architectural defect</note> unless a specific remedy is performed. **9** With the sun in the south and water and gems in the left/north, the place for wealth in the northeast and the place for waste <note>original: "vit-sthane", the latrine</note> in the southwest, the house is balanced. **2**

Regarding the consideration of the **kitchen** <term>pakashala: literally 'cooking-hall'</term> and the water-place:
The volume <note>pinda</note> should be multiplied by the numbers corresponding to the elephants and the planets, then divided in sequence by the name-factors. Using the cycles of the sun and moon, and adding the date <note>tithi</note>, the lifespan and prosperity of the house are determined. If the calculation matches the planets Rahu or Ketu, it brings certain outcomes. **22** 

One should seek stability in the foundation through these calculations. A result of zero in the remainder indicates a lack of foundation, while a remainder of three or five indicates stability and permanence.

### In the Vāstukutūhala
In the treatise titled **Vāstukutūhala** <note>A famous 16th-century work on architecture by Thakkura Pheru or similar traditions</note>, the length of the land is multiplied by the width. This area is then multiplied by various numerical factors: nine <note>Nanda</note>, twelve <note>Arka</note>, zero <note>Kha</note>, four <note>Ambudhi/Oceans</note>, and six <note>Rasa</note>. 

By dividing these products by the appropriate divisors—twelve <note>Martanda/Suns</note> for the month, twenty-seven for the constellations <note>Nakshatra</note>, and others—the architect determines the following qualities:

->**Income — Lunar Day — Weekday — Constellation — Union — Half-Day — Divisional Portion**<-
->**Āya — Tithi — Vāra — Nakshatra — Yoga — Karaṇa — Aṃśaka**<-

According to the **Rājaballabha** <note>A standard manual for architects attributed to Mandana</note>, these nine limbs—Income, Constellation, Expenditure, and the Divisional Portions—must be calculated in sequence. Divide respectively by the lunar day, the weekday, and the rising sign <note>Lagna</note>. The remainders resulting from these divisions are the specific "life-force" values of the house. **1** The length should also be multiplied by the breadth; what is said of one applies to the other. For a priest <note>Vipra</note>, a building is considered praiseworthy only when these calculations yield auspicious remainders. **2**

Some authorities state that building is auspicious when the Sun is in Gemini, but others say the middle of the year—specifically the months of Jyeshtha, Ashadha, and Cancer—is preferable. In the months of Bhadrapada, Leo, and Scorpio, or during the sun's passage through Capricorn and Aquarius in the months of Pausha and Magha, constructing a house is said to bring good fortune. However, there are differing opinions regarding the months of Virgo and Sagittarius. **17**

For a north-facing house, the ninth day of the bright fortnight is recommended. For a south-facing house, the calculation begins from the new moon. **18** When the Sun is in Pisces, or during "Empty" days <term>Riktā: the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, considered unsuitable for new beginnings</term>, one should avoid starting a house. Construction should ideally begin when the planets are in moveable signs. **19**

Regarding the placement of temples and houses, one must consider the position of **Rahu** <note>The celestial dragon/shadow planet whose 'head' and 'tail' rotate through the compass points</note>.
<margin>Directions 3</margin> 
The "mouth" of Rahu should be avoided. When the Sun is in Pisces, Leo, or Cancer, specific corners become auspicious or inauspicious for the initial digging. **20** 

A well <note>kūpa</note> placed in the wrong part of the Vastu layout leads to the destruction of the owner. If placed in the **Northeast** <note>Ishanya</note>, it brings nourishment, prosperity, and an increase in wealth. If placed in the West, it causes the loss of sons or the death of the wife. In other directions, it may cause fear, pain from enemies, or very little happiness. **21** 

One must carefully designate the locations for:
*   Bathing <note>snāna</note>
*   Cooking <note>pāka</note>
*   Sleeping <note>śayana</note>
*   The armory/tools <note>astra</note>
*   Grain storage <note>dhānya</note>
*   The treasury <note>bhāṇḍāra</note>
*   Worship of the deities <note>devatā</note>

If these are misplaced, losses will surely follow. In the center, there should be no heavy weight...

### Longevity and Planetary Positions
If Jupiter <term>Jiva</term>, the Sun <term>Arka</term>, and Venus <term>Bhriguja</term> are positioned in the fourth, ninth, and corner houses, the duration of life is a full century. If the Giver of Boons <note>referring to a benefic planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> is in the ascendant, and the Sun and Jupiter are in the fifth house, the lifespan is likewise one hundred years. **23** 

When Venus, the Moon, and the Sun are in the fourth house from the ascendant <term>Lagna</term>, and Jupiter is in a cardinal house <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, the resident attains a lifespan of a hundred years. If Jupiter is in the fourth, the Moon and Saturn are in the eleventh, and the Sun is in a different sign, the life remains long. **24** 

If Venus is in its sign of exaltation <note>Pisces</note> or in the ascendant, or if Jupiter is in the house of Death <note>the 8th house, here referred to as the house of Yama</note>, or if Saturn is in its exaltation <note>Libra</note> in the eleventh house, then a very long life is attained. **25** 

If even one planet is in its highest degree of exaltation in the seventh house, or if it is placed in a friendly sign, it makes the person long-lived. **26** 

### The Auspicious Building Cycles
By using the constellations of Dhruva, Indu, Harit, and the waters <note>referring to specific Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note> along with Jupiter, a "Kingdom of Sons" <note>a flourishing lineage</note> is established. If the lords of these signs are combined with the Sun and Moon, this cycle <term>Chakra</term> becomes a giver of bondage and grain <note>meaning it secures wealth and resources</note>. **27** 

With the strength of the hands <note>referring to specific measurements or planetary "rays"</note>, Jupiter, and Saturn in the fourth house, and Mars placed in the house of fire <note>the South-East</note>, it bestows wealth. When these are combined with the designated names on the correct day and hand-measurement, it brings happiness and sons. **28** 

Under the constellations of Ajaikapada <note>Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Ahirbudhnya <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and Pushya, if combined with Saturn on a "piercing" day <note>a day of harsh planetary aspects</note>, the house becomes afflicted or "wicked" <note>dushta</note>. **29** 

When the Sun is in a fixed sign, the fruit is stable. One should look to the corners... then, measured by eight parts <note>Gaja: elephant/eight</note>, there is happiness in the branches <note>the rooms or extensions of the house</note>. When the measurement of the body is in the middle of the house-lord's station, there is happiness. A wise person should always determine the door after examining this diagram. **30** 

### The Royal Arch Diagram
->**Now, the Torana Chakra for Kings**<-

A diagram is drawn with four channels <term>Nadi</term> in the shape of an arch <term>Torana</term>. It is used to calculate the King's nakshatra and the Sun's position during entry and exit. **1** 

At the base of the pillar <note>Stambha-mula</note> there are eight units; at the Lion Gate <term>Simha Dwara: the main ceremonial entrance</term> there are eight; in the middle there are eight; thus, there are thirty-two divisions in the four directions of the arch. **2** 

Where the Moon is during exit and where the Sun is during entry matters: The Moon presiding over entry and the Sun over exit is auspicious for crops. However, for travel, royal processions, or entering a prison, it is not beautiful <note>inauspicious</note>. **3** 

If the Moon and Sun are both positioned at the entry, and the planets of death <note>malefics</note> are at the exit, the result is mixed for both entry and exit. **4** 

If the Moon is joined with a cruel planet <term>Krura Graha: such as Mars or Saturn</term> during either entry or exit, on that day, the kings may face the loss of their treasury, their gates, or their royal queen. **5** 

The first part should be known as twofold...
