### **"Now, the Chapter on Entering a New House"**

During the northern solstice <term>Sun's northern course</term>, in the months of Jyeshtha, Tapasya <note>Phalguna</note>, or Madhava <note>Vaishakha</note>, and upon the king's return from a journey, the entry into a new house should take place. This should be done under the influence of gentle <term>Mridu</term>, fixed <term>Dhruva</term>, or swift <term>Kshipra</term> stars, and when the rising sign is a fixed one in an increasing house. **1** If Jupiter is in the ascendant, it destroys fear; the same is known for Mercury. <gloss>regarding the professional path</gloss> For men and women, the consideration of the "Wealthy Man" and "Wind" positions should be examined here. **2** Using gentle, fixed, swift, or mobile stars brings auspiciousness. <gloss>Auspiciousness is well-established</gloss> One should perform the sprinkling of water when the benefic planets are in the trines <term>1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term>, the center <term>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, or the house of gains <term>11th</term>, provided they are not occupied by malefic planets. **3** <gloss>When viewed favorably by all</gloss> Using the gentle, fixed, swift, or mobile stars, at a time free from the "empty" lunar days <term>Rikta Tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th</term>, the Sun’s influence, and the "toxic" period <term>Vishti</term>. <gloss>the name of the rising sign</gloss> <insert>First, having performed the rituals for Mercury and the Sun, one should focus on the auspicious compatibility. **4** With the Sun to the left, and placed in the eighth, fifth, second, or eleventh houses</insert> for a house facing east. On a "Full" lunar day <term>Purna Tithi</term>, it is auspicious for an east-facing house; for houses facing south, west, or north, the "Joyous" <term>Nanda</term> days are best. **5** At the time of entry <gloss>time of entry</gloss>, consider the "Pot Chart" <term>Kumbha Chakra: a diagnostic diagram for the house</term>. <insert>Created in four parts: success in the East, gain in the South, and wealth in the West.</insert> In the North, it leads to the destruction of the womb or the interior. The "Three" <gloss>from these</gloss> bring eternal stability when located in the "throat" of the diagram. **6** Thus, having entered one’s home at a good astrological moment, <gloss>on the altar</gloss> decorated with canopies and flowers <gloss>blossoms</gloss>, and accompanied by the sound of the Vedas <gloss>the chanting of the scriptures</gloss>, <insert>with a priest who knows the rites of the gods</insert> the King should honor the land with gifts of gold and clothing. **7** <gloss>There was</gloss>

<margin>
It is told that in the city of Dharmapura, there are those who study the six limbs of the Vedas <term>Vedangas</term>. He who possesses such knowledge and studies the scriptures is known as a scholar. **8** It is said that the *Chintamani* <note>this text</note> was created using calculations from birth charts <term>Jataka</term> and encyclopedias of omens <term>Samhita</term>. These various methods of the science of horoscopy and astronomical calculation are many.
</margin>

In the city of Dharmapura, which is adorned by Brahmins who have studied the six limbs of the Vedas, there lived the crown-jewel of astrologers, who achieved effortless mastery over the fruits of the stars. He was a respected creator of birth charts and astronomical tables for great kings. His intellect shone through his mastery of logic <term>Tarka</term>, rhetoric, and the sentences of the Vedas, and he was served by the virtuous. This leader of astrologers, worshipped by many, was like a lotus to the eyes. <gloss>his son</gloss> His second son, known by the name Ananta, became the foremost among his peers on this earth. He was like a beautiful mountain of knowledge, destroying the eight types of calamities. His commentary on the *Kamadhenu* calculations brought satisfaction to everyone. **2** <insert>His son, whose wisdom is for the enlightened</insert>

---

<margin>
At whatever planet the entry is desired, that direction becomes the "face" of the house. **4** Using the gentle and fixed stars, the entry should be made. **3** "Sun to the left" means: in the eighth, fifth, second, or eleventh houses. When entering a house in a city, if the Sun is in the fifth house, an entry toward the East is "to the left" and thus auspicious. Likewise, if the Sun is in the tenth house from the ascendant, the entry is from the front... if the Sun is in the second or fifth house, then the entry is from the West.
</margin>

<margin>
<gloss>He performed the duties of his station; likewise, his intellect shone through logic, rhetoric, and the Vedas. Logic refers to the science of justice; rhetoric to the science of poetic ornament; the sentences of the Vedas refer to the teachings of Mimamsa and Vedanta.</gloss> <insert>Dhramartha Trust J & K - An eGangotri-Vaidika Bharata Initiative</insert> <gloss>The son of the deity-like man, Ananta, attained great fame. This was the time. **2**</gloss>
</margin>

<margin>
In the eleventh house, and then the fifth house—these five positions are where the Sun should be situated for a house-entry to be auspicious. For one entering toward the North, the Sun should be to the left.
</margin>

...having placed the lotus feet of Ganesha in his heart, Rama <note>the author, Rama Daivajna</note> composed this in the excellent city of the Lord of the Mountains <note>Kashi/Varanasi, the city of Shiva</note> in the Shaka year 1522 <note>1600 CE</note>. May this *Muhurta Chintamani* <note>The Wish-Fulfilling Gem of Auspicious Moments</note> be at the forefront of the minds of the wise. **3** According to the *Rajavallabha* <note>a classical text on architecture</note>, one should not perform a house-warming on a Friday or a Wednesday if the house faces South. However, there is no defect in a house facing South if it is located in the same city as the owner's birth. According to the *Ratnamala* <note>The Garland of Jewels, an astrological treatise by Shripati</note>, the entry is auspicious when benefic planets are in the angles <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> or when the rising sign <term>Lagna</term> is a human sign <note>Gemini, Virgo, Libra, or the first half of Sagittarius</note> and a fixed sign. **9** Thus ends the eleventh chapter on the Section of Entering a New House in the *Muhurta Chintamani*, composed by the astrologer Rama, son of the astrologer Ananta. **11**

# Authority of the Heart Diagram

->Knowing the placement of the Moon and the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> therein, one may determine what is auspicious and inauspicious. This diagram was proclaimed by the wise Sage Garga.<- "Two stars should be placed at the seat, and two at the mouth. Four should be placed in the Southeast <note>the fire corner</note>, and four in the Southwest." **2** "Three should be given to the front, and four to the Northwest. Four should be known in the Northeast, and three shall be in the center." **3** "The seat brings all types of happiness; the mouth brings freedom from defects; the Southeast causes loss of wealth; the Southwest grants happiness." **4** "The front is highly excellent; the Northwest brings stars of success; the Northeast causes total loss; and the center results in the gain of wealth." **5** **This concludes the Heart Diagram.**

The Sun and Rahu <note>the north node of the moon</note> move through the three signs starting with Pisces, then the three starting with Scorpio, and the three starting with Virgo, respectively. **6** Regarding the Moon: it is auspicious in the East when in Sagittarius, Aries, or Leo. In the South, it is in Virgo, Capricorn, or Taurus. In the West, it is in Aquarius, Libra, or Gemini. In the North, it is in Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces. **7**

The lunar mansions of Pushya, Swati, Hasta, Shravana, Vishakha, and Anuradha, along with the groups related to the Vasus <note>Dhanishta</note> and the three "Northern" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, are considered auspicious for commerce and trade. This should be known as beneficial for profit and excellent for entering a new home. **8** Thus ends the twelfth chapter on the Section of Entering a New House in the *Muhurta Chintamani*, composed by the astrologer Rama, son of the astrologer Ananta. **12** 

**This *Muhurta Chintamani* is now complete.**

Vava, Balava, Kaulava, Taitila, Gara, and Vanija—these are said to be the seven movable <term>Karanas: half-lunar days</term>. **1** At the end of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight <note>the waning moon phase</note>, and in the two parts of the new moon, there occur the fixed halves: Shakuni, Chatushpada, Naga, and Kimstughna on the first half of the first lunar day. **2** Now, the lunar days are of two types starting from the first day of the bright fortnight. From the first to the seventh is for profit and influence, then the twelfth and so on. **3** Among these ten <term>Karanas</term> <note>excluding the inauspicious "Vishti" or "Bhadra" Karana</note>, when they are free of obstacles, one should perform the four-fold auspicious actions. **4** Thus, **it is said that the entry of a bride <note>original: "Vadhupravesham"</note> is not praised during the day, and the entry of a king is not praised at night.**
**Entering a house during the day or at night—all of this comprises the three-fold types of entry.** **1**

### Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,
#### Jammu

**No.** **83.6 - Gha** 
<note>original: "८३.६ - घ"; "Gha" is the fourth letter of the Sanskrit alphabet, likely serving as a shelf-mark or classification category within the library’s organizational system.</note>

**Title** **The Wish-Fulfilling Jewel of Auspicious Timing**
<note>original: "मुहूर्त्तचिन्तामणि" (*Muhurtachintamani*). This is a highly influential 16th-century treatise on <term>Muhurta</term> (electional astrology). The title metaphorically describes the text as a "Chintamani"—a mythological wish-fulfilling gem—because it provides the "jewels" of knowledge required to select the most cosmically favorable moments for various life activities.</note>

**Author** **Rama the Astrologer**
<note>original: "रामदैवज्ञः" (*Rama Daivajnah*). Rama was a prominent scholar of the 16th century. His title, <term>Daivajna</term>, literally means "knower of the divine" or "one who knows the will of the gods," which is the traditional Sanskrit term for an expert in astrology and astronomy.</note>

**Extent** **31** .................................. **Age** .........................
<note>original: "३१"; this indicates that the manuscript consists of 31 leaves or folios. The age of this specific copy was left blank on the label.</note>

**Subject** **Astrology** ............ **Complete**
<note>original: "ज्योतिषम् ............ सम्पूर्णम्" (*Jyotisham ............ Sampurnam*). <term>Jyotisha</term> is the ancient Indian science of time-keeping and celestial observation, encompassing both astronomy and astrology. The word "Sampurnam" indicates that this manuscript contains the full text of the work, rather than a fragment or a specific chapter.</note>

->**Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,**<-

**No.** T 3-6 - Gh <note>original: "ट ३-६ - घ"; the Sanskrit letters 'Ta' and 'Gha' are used here as shelf-marks or classification codes within the library's filing system.</note>

**Title** **The Thought-Gem of Auspicious Timing** <note>original: "मुहूर्त्तचिन्तामणि" (*Muhūrtacintāmaṇi*). Composed in the 16th century, this is one of the most authoritative texts on <term>Muhurta</term>, the branch of Vedic astrology dedicated to selecting the most favorable moments for weddings, journeys, and religious rituals.</note>

**Author** **Rama the Astrologer** <note>original: "रामदेवज्ञः" (*Rāmadaivajñaḥ*). Rama, the son of Ananta, was a celebrated scholar. His title <term>Daivajna</term> literally means "one who knows the divine [will]," the traditional term for a master of the astral sciences.</note>

**Extent** 31 [Folios] <note>original: "३१"; this indicates the manuscript consists of 31 individual leaves.</note> **Age** ...........................

**Subject** **Astrology** <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (*Jyotiṣam*). This term encompasses the entire tradition of Indian astronomy and astrology.</note> ........... **Complete** <note>original: "सम्पूर्णम्" (*Sampūrṇam*); this confirms that the manuscript contains the full text from beginning to end.</note>

Rama was a prominent scholar of the astral sciences; the title <term>Daivajna</term> literally means "one who knows the divine [will]" and was the standard professional designation for an astrologer-astronomer.</note>

**Subject** <insert>Astrology, Complete</insert> <note>original: "ज्योतिषम् सम्पूर्णम्" (*Jyotiṣam sampūrṇam*). <term>Jyotisha</term> refers to the traditional Indian system of astronomy and astrology. **Title** **The Thought-Jewel of Auspicious Moments** <note>original: "मुहूर्तचिन्तामणि" (*Muhurtachintamani*). ### Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,
### Jammu

**No.** 836 - Gha <note>original: "८३६ - घ"; the letter 'Gha' is the fourth consonant of the Sanskrit alphabet, likely indicating a specific shelf-mark, series, or classification category within the library.</note>

Composed by Rama Daivajna in 1525 CE, this is a foundational text in <term>Muhurta</term> (electional astrology), used to calculate the most spiritually and cosmically favorable times for rituals, weddings, and journeys.</note>

**Author** <term>Rama the Astrologer</term> <note>original: "Ramadaivajnah" (रामदैवज्ञः). A celebrated scholar from the lineage of Ananta Daivajna. The title <term>Daivajna</term> literally means "knower of fate" or "astrologer."</note>

<meta>This page continues the archival cataloging from the Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute. It provides the bibliographic details for a manuscript of the Muhurtachintamani, a standard text in Indian astrology.</meta>

### Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,
-> Jammu <-

**No.** T-36 — Gha <note>original: "ट३६ — घ"; the letter 'Ta' (ट) likely represents a shelf-mark or classification category, followed by the manuscript number 36 and the sub-category 'Gha'.</note>

**Title** <term>The Thought-Jewel of Auspicious Moments</term> <note>original: "Muhurtachintamani" (मुहूर्तचिन्तामणि). A seminal 16th-century treatise by Rama Daivajna, this work is the primary authority for selecting the most favorable times for rituals, ceremonies, and daily activities in the Hindu tradition.</note>

**Author** <term>Rama the Astrologer</term> <note>original: "Ramadaivajnah" (रामदेवज्ञः). Rama was a renowned scholar and a member of a distinguished family of astronomers and astrologers.</note>

**Extent** 31 <note>original: "३१"; this denotes that the manuscript is composed of 31 individual folios or leaves.</note> **Age** [Not specified]

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> — Complete <note>original: "Jyotisham sampūrṇam" (ज्योतिषम् सम्पूर्णम्). The term <term>Jyotisha</term> refers to the traditional Indian study of celestial bodies and time-keeping. This entry notes that the entire text of the work is preserved here.</note>
